# CEREBRO Patch Registry

Permanent inline modifications and fork-additions in upstream-zone files. Each entry
documents one named patch + its rationale + the file location(s).

**Marker format:** `// CEREBRO-PATCH(<name>): <description>` (or language-appropriate
comment for SQL/CSS/SBPL/JSON-with-_comment-field).

## Summary

- **Unique patch names:** 301 (273 baseline + 15 PR #118 markers + 6 JEH-725 markers + 1 JEH-1360 marker + 2 TECH-3100 terminal bug fixes + 2 FIR-2662 markers + 2 FIR-2730 scroll defaults)
- **Files marked (including chunks 4-8 pre-existing markers):** 302
- **Files newly marked in chunk 11:** 280 (chunk-11 scope)
- **Total marker lines added in chunk 11:** ~280 (one comment line per file)
- **Marker lines added in PR #118:** 16 (managed Firtal Gateway runtime scope)
- **Total fork-vs-upstream `+` lines (chunk-11 scope, across 280 files):** 22,170
- **Phase 4 target for cerebro-mod lines:** ≤200 (escalation threshold: >300)

> **Status: ESCALATED.** The 22,170 `+`-line count is far above the original Phase 4 target of ≤200.
> The original audit estimated 42 L3-marked files; reality includes ~280 upstream-zone files,
> the majority of which are net-new fork features (artifacts, profile, MCP, inbox-folders,
> work-sessions, project-access, budgets) sitting in upstream paths rather than `cerebro-*` packages.
> True L3 inline-modifications (where cerebro logic is woven into upstream files) are a small
> subset; the rest should ideally be RELOCATED to `cerebro-*` packages in a follow-up phase.
> See `Categories` section below.

## Categories

| Category | File count | Lines (`+`) | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| Net-new fork files in upstream paths | 126 | 17917 | Marker on file header; candidate for relocation to `cerebro-*` |
| True L3 inline modifications | 154 | 4253 | Marker on file header (or inline at modification site for chunks 4-8) |

| `transcript-safe-values` | packages/views/common/task-transcript/build-timeline.ts | 1 inline + helper in `packages/cerebro-sessions/task-transcript-safety.ts` | FIR-2942 — historical or drifted run-log rows can contain object-shaped fields where the transcript UI expects text (`tool`, `content`, `output`, `trigger_summary`). React then crashes with error #31 ("object with keys {}") when the blue run-log button opens the transcript. The upstream transcript builder now delegates normalization to a Cerebro-owned helper before rendering, preserving valid strings and JSON-stringifying malformed values instead of crashing. |
| `dialog-scroll-default` | packages/ui/components/ui/dialog.tsx (`DialogContent` default className gains `max-h-[calc(100dvh-2rem)] overflow-y-auto`) | 1 inline + marker | FIR-2730 — the shared `DialogContent` owned no height cap and no scroll, so any dialog whose content exceeded the viewport rendered centered at full intrinsic height and hung its top AND bottom off-screen with no scrollbar (measured: top −401px, bottom +401px in a 700px window; `overflow: visible`). Every dialog author had to remember a scroll area manually; when forgotten, content was clipped and unreachable — the recurring bug Jesper reported ("changes" review diff). This makes the DEFAULT safe: every tall dialog now caps to the viewport and scrolls. Zero per-modal changes needed; worst case is "footer scrolls with content" instead of "content off-screen". NOT flag-gated (pure layout safety default). |

Paired with the `SkillDiffView` `shrink-0` fix (cerebro zone) that stops the inner diff from being crushed by flex. NOT flag-gated (pure layout safety default). |

| `pdf-modal-inline-render` | packages/views/editor/attachment-preview-modal.tsx (4 marked import lines + flag/workspace hooks + `pdf` case swap)<br>packages/views/editor/attachment-preview-modal.test.tsx (zone-exempt test; mocks + flag-on/off coverage) | ~14 inline | FIR-2152 — a PDF attached to an issue **comment** only showed page 1: the comment `AttachmentPreviewModal` framed the file's *download* URL in a bare `<iframe>`, which Chromium renders as a degraded single-page preview. The full attachment page already renders every page via the cerebro `PdfViewer` (it fetches the bytes with credentials and frames a same-origin blob, bypassing the `Content-Disposition: attachment` / `frame-ancestors` limits). The modal now reuses that same `PdfViewer` for `full` PDF sources, gated by the existing `cerebro_pdf_inline_render` flag (default on; off → original single-page iframe). URL-only sources (copy-pasted across comments, no attachment record) keep the iframe since they lack the raw `download_url` needed to scope the credentialed fetch. The fileUrl is workspace-scoped via `attachmentDownloadHref(download_url, wsId)` — without `?workspace_id=` the credentialed fetch is rejected by the workspace-membership middleware on self-hosted (non-CDN) deploys, which is exactly Firtal's prod. Imports reuse the established phantom-dep pattern (`@multica/cerebro-artifacts/views/components`) already used by `project-detail.tsx` / `issue-detail.tsx`, so no `views→cerebro-artifacts` package edge is added (avoids the turbo build cycle the `artifact-mention-readonly` patch documents). |
| `due-date-shortcuts-format` | packages/views/issues/components/issue-detail.tsx (import line + 3 `DropdownMenuItem` onClick handlers) | 4 | FIR-2005 — the `Today`, `Tomorrow`, `Next week` shortcuts in the issue context menu (3-dot Due date submenu) called `new Date().toISOString()` / `d.toISOString()`, producing a full ISO timestamp (`2026-06-25T08:00:24Z`). The server requires date-only (`YYYY-MM-DD`) and rejected anything with a time component, returning "Failed to update issue". The sidebar `DueDatePicker` already used `toDateOnly()` correctly. Fix: import `todayDateOnly()` and `addDaysDateOnly(days)` from `@multica/core/issues/date` and use them in all three handlers. |
| `stacked-data-tables` | packages/ui/markdown/cerebro-stacked-tables.tsx (new fork file, upstream path, header marker)<br>packages/views/editor/readonly-content.tsx (import + rehype pipeline + sanitize schema + table className forward)<br>packages/views/editor/styles/cerebro-overrides.css (scope layout-table rules to `:not(.data-table)` + new data-table wrap/card rules)<br>packages/ui/markdown/Markdown.tsx (import + rehype pipeline + sanitize schema + table/td renderers in minimal & full modes)<br>packages/ui/markdown/markdown.css (mobile card rules)<br>packages/views/editor/cerebro-stacked-tables.test.ts (new, zone-exempt) | ~10 inline + new files | FIR-1727 — agent-authored markdown tables were unreadable across interfaces. On desktop cells never wrapped (one runaway line); on a phone four columns were crushed so narrow that words shredded letter-by-letter ("Kri sti an"). Both came from the two opposing layout-table CSS rules (`white-space:nowrap`+`min-width:max-content` for desktop scroll, force-fit `table-layout:fixed`+`overflow-wrap:anywhere` for mobile), which is why every prior tuning attempt (JEH-1611, JEH-707, FIR-2487) failed. A new rehype plugin tags real DATA tables (those with a `<thead><tr>` header row) with the `data-table` class and copies each column header onto its body cells as `data-label`; layout/email tables (no header row) are left on the existing scroll behaviour. CSS then (a) wraps data tables normally on desktop and (b) below 640px renders one labelled card per row. Applied to both markdown renderers (ReadonlyContent: comments/notes/artifacts/descriptions, and Markdown.tsx: chat). Jesper: "Jeg vil have tabeller som ser ordentlige ud på tværs af interfaces og både desktop og mobile." |
| `stacked-tables-editor` | packages/views/editor/cerebro-stacked-tables-extension.ts (new fork file, upstream path, header marker)<br>packages/views/editor/extensions/index.ts (import + register `StackedTablesExtension` after the Table nodes)<br>packages/views/editor/styles/cerebro-overrides.css (hide the editable data-table header row on mobile)<br>packages/views/editor/cerebro-stacked-tables-extension.test.ts (new, zone-exempt) | ~2 inline + new files | FIR-2482 — the `stacked-data-tables` rehype plugin only tagged tables in the READONLY markdown path, so the editable Tiptap editor (notes, issue descriptions, editable documents) rendered ProseMirror table nodes that never got the `.data-table` class. FIR-1727's follow-up (`editor-table-wrap`) patched desktop wrap only, leaving mobile on the column-cram rule that still shredded wide tables (Jesper's 7-column note screenshot). This adds an editable-editor extension mirroring the rehype logic as display-only ProseMirror decorations (no doc mutation, stored markdown untouched): every table with a header row is tagged `.data-table` and each body cell gets a `data-label` from its column header, so the existing mobile card CSS applies. One CSS rule hides the header row on mobile because ProseMirror emits header `<th>` inside `<tbody>` (no `<thead>`), which the thead-hiding card rule misses. |
| `stacked-tables-editor-wrapper` | packages/views/editor/styles/cerebro-overrides.css (new `@media (max-width:640px)` block keyed on `.tableWrapper.data-table`) | ~55 (CSS block, one CEREBRO-PATCH marker on header) | FIR-2482 follow-up — the `stacked-tables-editor` patch above never took effect in the editable editor. Its extension tags the table via a ProseMirror `Decoration.node({class:"data-table"})`, and ProseMirror applies a node decoration's class to the node's OUTER dom element. Tiptap wraps every editable table in a `.tableWrapper` div (columnResizing/tableView), so the class lands on the WRAPPER, not the inner `<table>`. Every card rule keyed off `table.data-table` therefore matched nothing and notes/descriptions still rendered a cramped multi-column grid (verified live on Sara.firtal.com at 390px). This re-applies the identical mobile card layout keyed off `.tableWrapper.data-table` (the wrapper where the class actually lands) scoped to `:not(.readonly)`; the readonly markdown path is untouched because rehype puts the class on `<table>` there. The per-cell `data-label` decoration already lands on each `<td>` correctly, so labels were present — only the block/card display was missing. |
| `chat-mermaid-html` | packages/ui/markdown/Markdown.tsx (new optional `renderCodeBlock` prop threaded into the minimal + full code renderers, mirroring the existing `renderImage`/`renderFileCard` override hooks)<br>packages/views/common/markdown.tsx (import `MermaidDiagram`/`HtmlBlockPreview` from `../editor/*` + supply `renderCodeBlock` that maps `mermaid`→diagram, `html`→preview)<br>packages/views/common/markdown.test.tsx (new, zone-exempt) | ~10 inline | FIR-2372 — agent chat / DM ("Talking to …") rendered fenced ```mermaid and ```html blocks as raw highlighted code (grey "MERMAID" label), while comments/notes/artifacts/channels already draw them as a real diagram / sandboxed HTML preview via `ReadonlyContent`. The chat surface uses the lighter `@multica/ui/markdown` renderer, which sent every fenced block to `CodeBlock`. Rather than duplicate the editor renderers, the base markdown gains a `renderCodeBlock({language,code})` override (same pattern as `renderImage`/`renderFileCard`, whose doc-comments state they exist to bridge chat to comment parity); the views wrapper supplies one that reuses the exact `MermaidDiagram` + `HtmlBlockPreview` components comments use. Returns null for every other language so normal code keeps its `CodeBlock` highlighting. Jesper: "Chatten skal også kunne rendere det samme som comments kan." |
| `agent-claude-context-footprint` / `agent-cursor-context-footprint` / `agent-pi-context-footprint` / `agent-opencode-context-footprint` / `agent-openclaw-context-footprint` / `agent-firtal-local-context-footprint` | server/pkg/agent/claude.go, cursor.go, pi.go, opencode.go, openclaw.go, firtal_local.go (each: capture last-turn footprint at its per-turn usage site + apply before returning usage)<br>New cerebro-zone files: server/pkg/agent/cerebro_context_footprint.go (shared `lastTurnFootprint` helper) + cerebro_context_footprint_test.go | ~3 inline per file | FIR-1870 — the context-window indicator read the cumulative cross-turn token sum as window occupancy. With prompt caching warm each turn re-reads the cached prefix, so the summed cache_read is several times the window and pinned the gauge at 100% (reported 3350k/1000k). FIR-1856 fixed this for Codex only; these runtimes over-count identically. Each now reports the last turn's prompt footprint (whole prompt incl. cache, in its own accounting) so the indicator shows real occupancy. Gemini/Copilot/Hermes/Kimi/Kiro are intentionally left on the cumulative fallback — their stream's token accounting cannot be verified from code, and a wrong footprint is worse than none. |

| `repo-url-trailing-slash` | server/internal/daemon/daemon.go (new `normalizeRepoURL` helper + applied in `repoAllowlist`, `workspaceRepoAllowed`, `registerTaskRepos`, `ensureRepoReady`'s initial trim)<br>server/internal/daemon/daemon_test.go (new `TestWorkspaceRepoAllowedIgnoresTrailingSlash`, marked) | ~4 inline across 4 call sites | FIR-2786 — `multica repo checkout https://github.com/firtal-group/warning-management-system` failed with "repo is not configured for this workspace" even though the repo was configured, because that repo's workspace-registered URL carries a trailing slash while every other configured repo's URL doesn't, and `workspaceRepoAllowed` did an exact string-map lookup with zero normalization. An earlier agent run misread this as "repo checkout is broken workspace-wide" (unverified — 8 other repos in the same workspace checked out fine). The fix normalizes trailing slashes at every point a repo URL is written into or read from the allow-map, so a repo's exact configured URL string no longer has to match the URL a caller passes byte-for-byte. Landed as a local patch (not proposed upstream first) because this workspace has no checkout access to `multica-ai/multica`. |
| `offline-parked-queued-grace` | server/pkg/db/queries/agent.sql (ExpireStaleQueuedTasks: 2 marked NOT EXISTS blocks + `@offline_grace_secs` param)<br>server/pkg/db/generated/agent.sql.go (regenerated; marker copied from SQL comment)<br>server/cmd/server/runtime_sweeper.go (1 marked const + 1 marked caller field)<br>server/cmd/server/runtime_sweeper_test.go (new `TestExpireStaleQueuedTasksGracesOfflineRuntime`) | ~20 inline | FIR-1722 — a comment-wake queued while the assignee's runtime was offline is *parked* (the wake is enqueued; the upstream `shouldEnqueueOnComment` gate does not check online-ness), but the generic queued-TTL sweeper (`ExpireStaleQueuedTasks`, MUL-1899) only spared `paused_at IS NOT NULL` runtimes — so a parked task on a merely *offline* runtime was deleted as `queued_expired` after 2h and never ran when the daemon reconnected (it reconnects on the same `runtime_id` via the `(workspace_id,daemon_id,provider)` upsert key, so the task would otherwise be claimable). This adds a bounded offline grace (24h) so parked work survives a normal offline window (e.g. overnight) and runs on reconnect, while a genuinely dead runtime's backlog still drains after the grace (agent-less offline runtimes are still reaped by `DeleteStaleOfflineRuntimes`). Paused runtimes keep their unbounded exemption. Directly addresses Jesper's "parkeret opgave genstarter ikke når maskinen kommer online igen". Companion to `wake-no-silent-fail` (FIR-1703), which made the same drop *visible*. |

Membership is DERIVED from timeline order against session start markers (`groupTimelineBySession`) — there is no per-comment field. P2: when `cerebro_session_activity_fold` is on, a session's activity blocks are split out (`partitionSessionGroups`) and tucked into one collapsed `SessionActivity` ("Activity (N)") fold below its comments. Both default OFF and no-op unless `cerebro_comment_chapters` is on; off restores the prior FIR-1741 render exactly. |

| `wakeup-parked-activity` | packages/views/issues/components/issue-detail.tsx (1 case + 1 icon + 1 coalesce-set entry + 1 import)<br>packages/views/locales/{en,ja,ko,zh-Hans}/issues.json (3 keys + comment each)<br>server/internal/cerebro/wakeup/service.go + queries/wakeup.sql (cerebro zone, marker optional)<br>apps/mobile/lib/format-activity.ts (outside upstream zone, no marker) | ~10 frontend | TECH-3734 — when a wakeup can't fire because the agent runtime is offline or the agent has no runtime, the dispatcher used to silently `postpone()` (push `fire_at` +5 min, write an `slog.Info`) with nothing visible to the user, looping every 5 minutes forever. The backend now records a `wakeup_parked` system activity on the **transition into the parked state** (first postpone in a streak — anti-flood, not every retry) carrying the human `reason` (`offline` / `no_runtime`) + `next_attempt_at`. `consecutive_postpones` was misnamed (it incremented on dispatch); it now counts actual postpones, resets to 0 on a successful dispatch, and crossing the loop threshold sends the existing inbox notification — so offline loops finally reach the owner. The frontend renders the new action via a `formatActivity` case (reason-aware label), a `BellOff` lead icon, and a `NEVER_COALESCE_ACTIONS` entry; mobile mirrors the label. Relates to TECH-2476 (the circuit-breaker that stops the loop itself). |

Approval wakeups are excluded — they always carry real work. |

The `[WAKEUP]` branch now leads with a positive mandate: the wakeup note is a REAL task scheduled for this moment; execute it and actively re-verify live state before concluding it is done. | `inbox-badge-thread-aware` | packages/views/layout/app-sidebar.tsx (marked import + hook swap, dead inbox query removed)<br>packages/views/inbox/components/inbox-page.tsx (marked import + 1 hook swap)<br>packages/views/platform/use-desktop-unread-badge.ts (marked import + 1 hook swap)<br>packages/cerebro-inbox/use-inbox-unread-count.ts (new, cerebro zone) | ~5 inline | FIR-2382 — the "Inbox" badge counted more unread than the inbox list could show. The badge counted every unread `inbox_item` (deduped by issue), but with `cerebro_inbox_thread_split` on (default) a channel/DM reply inside a thread carries `details.thread_root_id` and is deliberately excluded from the channel's server-side unread count (`CountUnreadInboxForChannelExcludingThreads`) — so it surfaces on its own thread row in the dynamic inbox and NOT at all in the classic inbox (Firtal's default). Result: an unread thread reply inflated the badge with no visible row. The three client badge surfaces (sidebar, inbox header, desktop dock) now call `useCerebroInboxUnreadCount`, which drops thread replies before deduplicating — but only when the same flag drives the server exclusion, so badge and channel row always agree. The badge is a count of unread CONVERSATIONS; a thread reply has independent Slack-style read state and is not part of it. Known follow-ups (not in this PR, both default-off/secondary surfaces): the cross-workspace OS app-icon badge (`CountUnreadInboxForUserAllWorkspaces`) and the smart-unread axis (`cerebro_channel_unread_smart`, default off). |

Previously the watchdog re-dispatched a stalled sub-issue once, then gave up and froze the flow waiting for a human (the failure Jesper hit: an agent ran out of usage mid-task and the whole orchestration never reached the goal). Now the recovery ladder is bounded re-dispatch → hand-off → human: on the second stall the watchdog hands the sub-issue to the squad leader (a backup builder, via `handStalledChildToLeader`) to take over and finish, marks it `orch-handed-off`, and only escalates to a human if it is STILL stalled after that. Markers cleared by `clearStallMarkers` when the child recovers/terminates so a future stall re-runs the full ladder. |

Without this, the orphan-recovery / `HandleFailedTasks` path keeps minting retry rows on a paused runtime — `ClaimTask` rejects each one immediately with `runtime_paused`, so no LLM is called and no spend occurs, but the queue (and the UI's "retries being attempted" surface) fills with dead rows. Tine's runtime accumulated 299 such rows in a single rate-limit window. Lookup error is fail-safe: skip retry rather than risk a runaway on unknown pause state. Complements `auto-pause-on-failure` which only fires on the synchronous `FailTask` path; this guard covers the orphan / sweeper path that calls `MaybeRetryFailedTask` directly. |
| `suppress-serverside-timeout-blocked` | server/internal/service/task.go<br>server/internal/service/task_timeout_comment_test.go | 3 inline + test | FIR-2609 — the serverside stale-task sweeper (`FailStaleTasks`) sets `error="task timed out"` with `failure_reason=timeout` for two situations folded into one string: a still-`dispatched` task a paused/offline/busy runtime never started, and a `running` task that started but stalled. Only the first is bookkeeping noise (the runtime was away — the agent never ran), and both timeout-comment firing paths (`FailTask` + `HandleFailedTasks`) posted a scary "BLOCKED: Agent-run timed out" comment for it (the dispatched/never-started case is the dominant share of the 703 "task timed out" errors / 16.3% of all real errors — the primary user-reported noise in FIR-2340). `isUnstartedServersideTimeout(task, errMsg)` keys on a missing `started_at` to gate both `createAgentComment` calls: a never-started dispatch-timeout is now silent (run-log only), while a genuinely started-then-stalled run still surfaces BLOCKED (preserving FIR-2395 Case C `TestTimeoutPostsBlockedCommentForTrulySilentHang`), as do agent-internal timeouts ("`<agent> timed out after <dur>`") — all subject to the FIR-2395 already-replied gate. |

| `daemon-per-tool-call-timeout` | server/internal/daemon/config.go<br>server/internal/daemon/config_test.go<br>server/internal/daemon/daemon.go<br>server/internal/daemon/daemon_test.go | ~140 | FIR-2610 — per-tool-call hard cap (`MaxToolCallDuration`, env `MULTICA_MAX_TOOL_CALL_DURATION`, default 30m, 0=disable). The idle watchdog deliberately stands down while a tool call is in-flight (a long build emits no messages), so a tool call that hangs forever (a `sleep`/`until` poll loop, a frozen network call, an unresponsive MCP server) sat at "running" until the coarse 2h `DefaultAgentTimeout`. This cap force-stops it in minutes via a dedicated `tool_timeout` disposition ("tool call exceeded MaxToolCallDuration"). **Not cerebro-specific — a genuine upstream gap; fork-carried ONLY until upstream PR multica-ai/multica#3574 merges, then dropped on the next sync.** Exceeds the 5-line guideline because the cap is woven into the existing watchdog goroutine / drain loop and cannot be cleanly isolated into a cerebro-zone module without diverging from the upstream form it's meant to become; kept inline per Jesper's direction on FIR-2610 (approver: Jesper Hvejsel, 2026-05-31). |

| `chat-bubble-wrap-long-words` | packages/views/chat/components/chat-message-list.tsx | 5 | FIR-2560 — long URLs / unbreakable tokens in user and assistant chat content forced horizontal scroll on narrow chat panels (Mia floating chat on mobile width). Fix is two-part: (1) `min-w-0` on the user bubble's outer flex wrapper so the flex item can shrink below intrinsic content width and actually respect `max-w-[80%]`; (2) `wrap-anywhere` (overflow-wrap: anywhere) on the bubble + every assistant prose container so a long URL lowers min-content and breaks inside the column instead of pushing layout wider than the viewport. `break-words` (overflow-wrap: break-word) alone was insufficient because it does not adjust min-content sizing on flex children. |
| `channels-mobile-col-min-width` | packages/views/channels/components/channel-detail.tsx | 2 | TECH-3490 — the DM/Channel message column (`relative flex flex-1 min-h-0 flex-col`) had no `min-w-0`, so on mobile it could not shrink below its content's intrinsic width and rendered 434px wide inside a 390px viewport — clipping every message line at the right edge (Jesper's Jakob DM screenshot). The earlier markdown-overflow-guard / editor overflow-wrap rules never applied here because channel messages render through `ReadonlyContent` (`.rich-text-editor`), a different surface than `.markdown-content`. Verified live at 390px: column 434→390, document overflow count many→0. Adds `min-w-0`. |
| `markdown-overflow-guard` | packages/ui/markdown/markdown.css | 2 | TECH-3490 — completes FIR-2560. `wrap-anywhere` was placed on the chat bubble/prose containers, but `.markdown-content` itself carries the `break-words` utility (overflow-wrap: break-word), which the actual text nodes (`p`/`a`/`code`) inherit — and break-word does NOT lower min-content. Verified live at 390px: the inbox chat scroll container measured scrollWidth 471 vs clientWidth 390 (81px horizontal overflow), user bubble 381px vs its 280px cap; after the rule, 0px overflow and bubble 280px. Sets `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on `.markdown-content` + its text descendants so long URLs/tokens break at the source on every markdown surface (chat, comments, descriptions, skills), not just chat. Unlayered import wins over the Tailwind utility layer. |

| `issue-context-bundle` + `issue-context-bundle-route` + `issue-context-cli` + `daemon-bundled-saving` + `daemon-bundled-prompt` + `daemon-task-bundle-context-hint` + `agent-task-bundle-context-hint` | server/cmd/server/router.go<br>server/cmd/multica/cmd_issue.go<br>server/internal/daemon/prompt.go<br>server/internal/daemon/types.go<br>server/internal/handler/agent.go<br>server/internal/handler/daemon.go | ~14 | FIR-2384 step 2 — the `bundled_read` cost saving made real for daemon agents. New `GET /api/issues/{id}/context` returns issue + recent thread + members + labels in ONE call, exposed as `multica issue context <id>`. When the saving is "on" the claim handler sets `BundleContextHint` and the start prompt points the agent at the single bundled call; the real save is measured at the endpoint per task (counts actual usage, not an assumed save). "shadow" records the would-save at claim time without changing behaviour; snapshot_prompt takes precedence so the two never double-count. Net-new logic + measurement live in cerebro-zone files (server/internal/handler/issue_context_cerebro.go, server/internal/handler/daemon_cost_bundled_cerebro.go, server/internal/daemon/cost_bundled_cerebro.go); these are the irreducible upstream wiring edits (route, CLI command, Task/response field, prompt guards, claim-handler call). |
| `daemon-graphify-nudge` + `agent-task-graphify-nudge` | server/internal/daemon/prompt.go<br>server/internal/daemon/types.go<br>server/internal/handler/agent.go<br>server/internal/handler/daemon.go | ~9 | FIR-1311 / FIR-1312 — adoption nudge for the `graphify` cost saving. When a workspace turns graphify "on", the claim handler ships a standing "use the code graph for navigation; self-install with `uv tool install graphifyy`" instruction in the new `GraphifyNudge` field; the daemon inlines it near the top of the start prompt for issue/comment/wakeup tasks, reaching ALL local runtime types. A DEDICATED field (not `IssueSnapshot`) is used on purpose — it must not claim the issue was pre-fetched and must be independent of snapshot_prompt/bundled_read. No measurement yet (deferred per FIR-1312); the graphify saving mode (off by default) is the kill switch. Net-new logic lives in cerebro-zone files (server/internal/handler/daemon_cost_graphify_cerebro.go, server/internal/daemon/cost_graphify_cerebro.go); these are the irreducible upstream wiring edits (Task/response field, prompt guards, claim-handler call). |
| `runtime-config-snapshot` + `runtime-config-bundled` | server/internal/daemon/execenv/execenv.go<br>server/internal/daemon/execenv/context.go<br>server/internal/daemon/execenv/runtime_config.go<br>server/internal/daemon/daemon.go | ~12 | FIR-2384 steps 1+2 follow-up (Tine QA) — the start prompt was rewritten for `snapshot_prompt`/`bundled_read`, but the standing runtime workflow brief (`buildMetaSkillContent`) still ordered the agent to run `multica issue get` + `multica issue comment list` separately, so the agent re-fetched and the saving stayed dead (and, for bundled_read, the per-task endpoint measurement never fired). Two `TaskContextForEnv` flags (`IssueSnapshotInlined`, `BundleContextHint`) carry the active saving into the brief; the read steps now point at the inlined snapshot (snapshot) or the single `multica issue context` call (bundled). snapshot_prompt has precedence. Default (both off) output is byte-for-byte unchanged. Step text lives in the cerebro-zone file server/internal/daemon/execenv/runtime_config_fewer_calls_cerebro.go. |

The per-turn prompt path (`daemon.buildCommentPrompt`) already called the hints; the brief path (`commentThreadStep` in the FIR-2384 cerebro wrapper) still emitted the old generic step 3, so `TestCommentTriggeredBriefCarriesNewCommentsHint` and `TestCommentTriggeredBriefResumedNoDeltaSkipsDefaultThreadRead` failed after the upstream sync. Hint selection (warm-with-delta → resumed-no-delta → cold) is now plumbed through `commentThreadStep` while the snapshot/bundle branches still win when those savings are active. |

The row re-sorts to its natural position only after the message is closed (selection cleared). Logic lives in `@multica/cerebro-inbox` (`pin-selected.ts`); the upstream `mergedEntries` sort delegates to `sortInboxEntriesPinned` and adds `selectedKey` to its deps. |
| `inbox-pin-selected-group` | packages/views/inbox/components/inbox-page.tsx | 5 small touchpoints | FIR-2474 (follow-up) — extends the pin to the GROUPED views. The original `inbox-pin-selected` only froze the open row's sort *time*, which holds it in place in the flat time-sorted list but not when the inbox is grouped (Group by → Action / Project / Agent / Type — Action is the default). When grouped, every row is bucketed live from signals that change with activity (run state, mentions, read/unread), so incoming activity moved the open row to a different group. This patch freezes the open row's bucket per group-mode at open time so it stays in the same group until closed (or until the user deliberately changes the grouping). Logic lives in `@multica/cerebro-inbox` (`pin-selected-group.ts`, `pinnedBucketizer`); the upstream `groupedEntries` memo delegates both its primary and secondary `bucketize` calls through it and adds `selectedKey` to its deps. |
| `inbox-action-grouping` | packages/views/inbox/components/inbox-page.tsx | ~11 small touchpoints | FIR-2115 — adds a "Group by → Action" inbox grouping that buckets entries by what to do next: **Unread** (any unread inbox item), **Running** (agent currently running on the issue/chat), **Done** (terminal issue status: done/cancelled, or read non-issue chat/channel), **Waiting** (open issue thread you've already seen, but which is not running or done yet). Buckets render in fixed precedence order (not alphabetical) via an optional `order` on `Group`, with Waiting last. Default grouping for new users, gated by `cerebro_inbox_action_grouping` (snaps back to "none" when disabled). All classification, labels, and ordering live in `@multica/cerebro-inbox` (`action-groups.ts` + `strings.ts`); the upstream page only delegates via `bucketizeInboxAction` / `useInboxActionGroupLabels` / `INBOX_ACTION_GROUP_BY_OPTION`. |

Routed through Tiptap's `autofocus` option (not a post-mount RAF) because `immediatelyRender: false` defers editor creation past any effect that calls `editor.commands.focus()` — the call no-ops because `editor` is `null`. | `issue-mention-new-tab` | packages/views/editor/readonly-content.tsx | 28 | JEH-874 + JEH-1048 + JEH-1112 — clicking an issue mention chip in a comment or description opens it in a new tab on desktop/wide-web via `openInNewTab(path)` / `window.open(path)`, with `href={path}` (relative). Earlier revisions used `getShareableUrl(path)` so right-click → Copy Link returned a full https:// URL; on cerebro deployments the desktop `appUrl` default (`https://multica.ai` in `apps/desktop/src/shared/runtime-config.ts`) made every chip visibly point at multica.ai instead of the host the app is actually served from, so the relative path is preferable. Copy-Link now returns a relative path. On mobile (`useIsMobile()` true) the chip falls back to SPA `push(path)` because the PWA renders in `display: standalone` (no tab UI) and `target="_blank"` either navigates the current window (iOS) or breaks out into the system browser (Android), both of which lose the thread context. Browser-back restores scroll + tiptap draft via the Next.js router cache. |

| `task-token-drop-fork-059` | server/migrations/108_task_token.up.sql | 4 | 2026-05-27 upstream sync — the fork's own per-task token (JEH-324, migration 059, `token_hash BYTEA` key) is superseded by upstream's MUL-2600 model (migration 108, `id`+`token_hash TEXT`+`user_id`). Both create a `task_token` table, so 108's bare `CREATE TABLE` would fail where 059 already ran (prod). A `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS task_token;` ahead of 108's CREATE recreates it in upstream's shape; safe because task tokens are short-lived. Approved by Jesper (FIR-2346). |

| `reconnect-timeline-invalidate` | packages/core/realtime/use-realtime-sync.ts | 2 | FIR-1941 — the WS reconnect sweep only invalidates workspace-scoped query keys (`["issues", wsId, ...]`, inbox, etc.), but the per-issue timeline cache is keyed `["issues", "timeline", issueId]` and was never refreshed. A `comment:created` event dropped during an offline window left the timeline marked "fresh" (default `staleTime: Infinity`), so clicking the inbox notification opened the issue without the new comment. The patch invalidates the `["issues", "timeline"]` prefix inside `invalidateWorkspaceScopedQueries`, so reconnect and workspace-switch both mark all timelines stale; the active observer refetches immediately and inactive ones refetch on next mount. |
| `inbox-wakeup-realtime` | packages/core/realtime/use-realtime-sync.ts | 2 | FIR-1677 — the inbox "Group by → Action" feature groups an issue with a pending agent wakeup under "Running" (via `cerebro_inbox_wakeup_running`), reading `useInboxWakeupStates` → query `["cerebro-inbox-wakeups", wsId]`. That query had `staleTime: 30_000` and **no** invalidation anywhere (there is no `wakeup:*` WS event). An agent typically schedules its next wakeup as it finishes a run: the live-run query refetches on the `task:*` event and the row leaves "live running", but the wakeup query stays stale, so the row fell to "Waiting" instead of staying in "Running". The patch invalidates `["cerebro-inbox-wakeups", wsId]` inside `invalidateTaskLifecycleQueries` (right beside `inbox-active-run-realtime`), so the wakeup refetches at the same moment. Key literal mirrored from `packages/cerebro-inbox/use-inbox-wakeups.ts` (core cannot import cerebro). |
| `reconnect-wakeup-invalidate` | packages/core/realtime/use-realtime-sync.ts | 2 | FIR-1677 — companion to `inbox-wakeup-realtime`. The reconnect/workspace-switch sweep in `invalidateWorkspaceScopedQueries` only refreshes workspace-scoped keys; the inbox wakeup list has its own top-level key `["cerebro-inbox-wakeups", wsId]` (not under `inboxKeys.all`), so a wakeup scheduled during an offline window never surfaced after reconnect. The patch invalidates that key on reconnect (same gap/shape as `reconnect-timeline-invalidate` directly above). |
| `inbox-active-run-realtime` | packages/core/realtime/use-realtime-sync.ts | 2 | JEH-1425 / FIR-2215 — the inbox run pip (the "running now" indicator on a row) reads `inboxKeys.activeIssueTasks(wsId)`. The live `task:` lifecycle handler must invalidate that key so an agent starting/finishing a run flips the pip without a refresh. The upstream cherry-pick #1856 inlined the `task:` handler and dropped this line (it survived only inside the unused `invalidateTaskLifecycleQueries`); restored beside `inbox-wakeup-realtime`. Guarded by `use-realtime-sync-cerebro-regression.test.tsx`. |
| `channel-unread-detail-sync` | packages/core/realtime/use-realtime-sync.ts | 5 | JEH-1249 / FIR-2215 — channel & DM inbox rows track unread via `channelKeys.list(wsId)` / `channelKeys.detail(wsId, issueId)`. The `comment:created/updated/deleted` handlers must invalidate them so a new channel/DM message flips the row to unread live. Upstream cherry-pick #1856 stripped these calls, so rows stayed "read" until a refresh; restored via the `invalidateChannelList` helper + a detail invalidate on create. Guarded by `use-realtime-sync-cerebro-regression.test.tsx`. |
| `realtime-regression-test` | packages/core/realtime/use-realtime-sync-cerebro-regression.test.tsx | new | FIR-2215 — renders `useRealtimeSync` against a mock WS and asserts the three patches above are wired (`registerCerebroHandlers` called + torn down; `task:*` invalidates the inbox run-pip + wakeup queries; `comment:created` invalidates channel list + detail). Fails loudly if a future upstream merge silently removes them again. |

| `cerebro-orchestration` | server/cmd/multica/main.go | 2 | FIR-2564 — register the `multica orchestrate` command (GroupID + AddCommand). The command and the engine it drives are net-new cerebro-zone code (`server/cmd/multica/cerebro_orchestrate.go`, `server/internal/cerebro/orchestration/`): a dependency-aware task-plan engine ported from the open-source codexmate pattern. Computes execution waves (Kahn topo-sort), rejects cycles/missing/self deps before running, runs ready nodes up to a concurrency limit with a single-writer lock (a `write` node runs exclusively), blocks downstream on failure, and retries per node. `run` executes a local simulation (--dry-run) today; wiring `executeNode` to live agent dispatch / sub-issue promotion is the gated server-side step. `orchestrate run --issue <id>` is label-gated on `orchestrate` so the engine stays inert on every issue until a human attaches the label — that is the early-rollout safety guarantee, not a feature flag. |

| `skill-skillmd-collision-guard` | server/internal/daemon/execenv/context.go | 5 | A Multica skill stores its `SKILL.md` body in the `content` field; `writeSkillFiles` writes that to `<dir>/SKILL.md`, then iterates the bundled supporting files. Real cases: the `prd-runbook` and `skills-runbook` workspace skills. The guard skips any bundled file whose cleaned path is `SKILL.md`, since `content` already owns that path. | `daemon-ws-write-accessor-signal-order` | server/internal/daemon/wakeup.go | 2 | TECH-3100 — `signalTaskWakeup` was called before `d.wsWrites.Store(&writes)`. A task goroutine could start immediately on the signal and call `cerebroAttachTerminal` while `wsWrites` was still nil, causing the `term_attach` frame to be silently dropped and no terminal session to ever be created ("Waiting for agent…" forever). | `daemon-cerebro-term-buffer` | server/internal/daemon/daemon.go<br>server/internal/daemon/cerebro_terminal.go<br>server/internal/daemon/wakeup.go | ~95 | TECH-3388 — the live terminal rides only the daemonws WS, which Cloudflare flaps every ~30-90 min (`close 1001 "CloudFlare WebSocket proxy restarting"`). Before, `cerebroTermPump.flush` dropped every `term_stdout` frame while the WS was down (`term_stdout dropped: ws not connected`), so output during the ~30s gap was lost and the terminal went blank. Fix: a per-task ordered backlog (`cerebroTermBuffers`) keeps the output; `sendOrBufferTermStdout` buffers when the WS can't send (preserving order behind any pending backlog), and the WS reconnect path in `wakeup.go` calls `drainAllCerebroTermBuffers` to replay each task's backlog in order after re-emitting the attach frames. Bounded at `cerebroTermBufferMaxBytes` (512 KB) per task, oldest-dropped-first. Does NOT add an HTTP fallback — it survives flaps, not a permanently-down WS. |

Fix: raise to `128 * 1024`. |

| `editor-mobile-table-wrap` | packages/views/editor/styles/cerebro-overrides.css | 4 inline (in a `@media (max-width:640px)` block) | JEH — email/message bodies pack prose into fixed-width layout tables; on a phone there is no room to scroll one sideways, so the row reads as "text cut off the right edge" and the user thinks text disappears. Below the mobile breakpoint, tables get `table-layout: fixed; width: 100%; min-width: 0; max-width: 100%` and their cells `white-space: normal; overflow-wrap: anywhere; width: auto` — all `!important` so they beat the **inline** `width="600"`/`style="width:680px"` that email clients emit. The table fits the column and wraps instead of forcing horizontal scroll. Desktop keeps the `min-width: max-content` + `white-space: nowrap` scroll behaviour from `editor-mobile-table-overflow`. Verified on WebKit (iPhone engine) at 390px: a 1212px-wide layout table collapses to 332px and reads fully. |
| `editor-table-wrap` | packages/views/editor/styles/cerebro-overrides.css | ~6 inline | FIR-1727 follow-up — the `stacked-data-tables` fix only reaches the readonly markdown renderers, because they tag tables `.data-table` via a rehype plugin. The EDITABLE Tiptap editor (note/document body, issue description, comment composer) renders real ProseMirror table nodes that never get that class, so they fell under the layout-table rule (`min-width:max-content`+`white-space:nowrap`) and on desktop ran off the right edge — the last column scrolled out of view (Jesper's note screenshot). The editable editor (`.rich-text-editor` WITHOUT `.readonly`) only ever holds user-authored data tables, never email layout tables, so every table there now wraps to fit: `width:100%; table-layout:auto; min-width:0` + cells `white-space:normal; overflow-wrap:break-word`. Mobile already wraps via the existing `:not(.data-table)` 640px rule, so this only fixes desktop. |

| `status-slack-dot` | packages/views/agents/presence.ts (5 values + doc)<br>packages/views/runtimes/components/runtimes-page.tsx (1 map)<br>packages/views/runtimes/components/shared.tsx (1 map)<br>packages/views/agents/components/inspector/runtime-picker.tsx (3)<br>packages/views/agents/components/runtime-picker.tsx (1)<br>packages/views/squads/components/squad-detail-page.tsx (1 map)<br>packages/views/onboarding/steps/step-runtime-connect.tsx (1)<br>packages/views/onboarding/components/compact-runtime-row.tsx (1) | ~16 inline across 8 upstream files | TECH-3686 — **supersedes the earlier `status-red-green` (TECH-3583)**. Jesper feedback + Slack screenshot: red as the inactive marker is too alarming; do it the Slack way. Every presence/status dot is now a Slack-style indicator — a filled centre on a thin grey outline ring (`border border-muted-foreground`). State reads from the centre fill, not colour: `bg-success` (green) online, `bg-warning` (amber) for transient `unstable`/`recently_lost`, and **`bg-background` (empty/hollow centre) for every previously-red inactive state** (paused/offline/archived/about_to_gc). Distinction stays at-a-glance (filled green vs hollow), which is what TECH-3583's red was protecting — now via shape, not red. The central agent config is `availabilityConfig` in `presence.ts` (propagates to ~10 agent-presence consumers); the rest are runtime-health maps and inline `isOnline ? : ` ternaries. Inactive runtime-health *tones* also drop red → `bg-muted text-muted-foreground`. Mirrored outside the upstream zone (no marker needed) in `apps/mobile/components/ui/presence-dot.tsx`, `apps/desktop/src/shared/daemon-types.ts` (`stopped` only — the `cli_not_found`/`auth_expired` error states keep red), and `packages/cerebro-inbox-slack-block/components/slack-block.tsx` (human presence dot). Deliberately NOT changed: `apps/web/features/landing/components/features-section.tsx` (marketing demo — its `Error`/`Offline` states are a stylised landing-page mock, not the live presence dots). |

The flat tab strip is regrouped into three collapsible sections (`Activity` / `Context` / `Advanced`, Advanced collapsed by default) rendered by the Cerebro-owned `AgentTabSections`; the upstream pane only resolves tab labels and hands the visible-tab list to it. FIR-2280: each section is a **self-contained tabbed card** — it remembers its own shown tab and renders that tab's content inside its own card (tab strip + content as one box), so **all three cards display their own tabs + content at once** (the requested `Tabs / Indhold` per-card layout). The pane passes a `renderContent(tabId)` function so each card can render its own; it still owns one "focus" tab (sibling nav-intent + the unsaved-changes guard), and when that changes the owning section is pointed at it and opened. (Earlier FIR-2280 builds used a single globally-active tab with one shared content node, so only one card carried content and the other two showed bare tab strips — "three rows of tabs"; this followup fixed that.) The `Instructions` tab renders the versioned Agent Office surface (`CerebroAgentContextTab`: history / propose / approve / diff / restore), replacing the plain instructions editor — instructions are edited through Propose change. All layout + section config live in `@multica/cerebro-agent-context`. |

Runtime bindings are read from the agent's actual runtime row (`GetAgentRuntimeForWorkspace` → `normalizedRuntimeCapabilities`), with the static provider registry as the baked-in fallback. Status mirrors the secret-set discipline (`known` / `not_configured` when the agent ran nothing / `unknown` on lookup failure), using `CountAgentTasksInWindow` to tell "ran nothing" from "ran but logged no tools". | `daemon-version-probe-cache` | server/internal/daemon/cerebro_version_probe.go + server/internal/daemon/cerebro_version_probe_test.go (net-new files)<br>server/internal/daemon/daemon.go (1 struct field, 1 init line, 2 call-site swaps) | net-new + ~4 inline | TECH-3647 — the daemon shells out to `<cli> --version` to detect each agent's CLI version, on two hot paths: per workspace at runtime registration (`registerRuntimesForWorkspace`) and per heartbeat tick per runtime (`refreshAgentVersions`). With many workspaces on one host the per-tick probes fan out across goroutines and run concurrently. On sara.local the hermes `--version` blocked ~14s (SQLite `state.db` lock + embedded update-check); the slow probes stacked across runtimes and pinned the host at load ~24, starving the staging deploy. A host-local shim short-circuiting `--version` was the emergency fix; this is the durable fix that lets the shim be removed. New `versionProbeCache` (`detectAgentVersionCached`) applies three independent mitigations: (1) memoise a successful probe per (provider, exec path) with a 10-min TTL so steady state is a map lookup; (2) run every probe under an 8s context deadline so a hung CLI never blocks a tick; (3) a daemon-wide concurrency cap of 1 so cache-miss bursts can't stack. Degrades gracefully — a timed-out/errored probe serves the last known-good version instead of wiping it off the heartbeat. Both call sites route through the cache; the `detectAgentVersion` package seam is preserved for test stubbing. |

The runtime calls Multica (publicly reachable, on Sliplane); the top-level `/mcp-relay/{name}` handler verifies the token, loads the connection server-side, and reverse-proxies (FlushInterval -1 for SSE) over the internal path with the connection's real credentials. The local machine never holds the connection secret and never touches the internal address — "kald gennem Multica, ikke på andre måder". Unconfigured → direct path unchanged (no regression). The orphan div stays glued to the page and, because the one shared `MermaidDiagram` renders every surface (comments, notes, artifacts, chat, code blocks), it surfaced everywhere. `suppressErrorRendering: true` makes `render()` call `removeTempElements()` before re-throwing, so no orphan is left; the component's own in-place error box (below the effect) still renders. |

| `skills-mobile-scroll` | packages/views/skills/components/skills-page.tsx (3 marked layout regions: page root, content section, list wrapper + inner column) | 3 marked className regions in 1 upstream file | FIR-2748 — the Skills page could not scroll vertically on mobile. The desktop app-shell layout traps the page at viewport height (`min-h-0` chain) and lets only the inner DataTable scroll, so on a phone the header + personal alert + filter rows ate the screen and the list scrolled in a cramped ~500px pane (Jesper: "kan ikke scrolle, useless" / "siden skal også kunne scrolle vertikalt"). The patch is mobile-first responsive: below `sm` the page root becomes the scroll container (`overflow-y-auto`), the content section drops `min-h-0`, and the list wrapper + inner column size to their content (the inner column switches to `block` flow so the shared DataTable's `flex-1 min-h-0` cannot collapse in an auto-height parent) — so the whole page scrolls. At `sm+` every original class is restored via `sm:` variants, leaving desktop behaviour unchanged. No shared DataTable/shell edit. |

| `daemon-report-context-footprint` + `daemon-report-context-footprint-guard` | server/internal/daemon/daemon.go (1 marked block: forward `ContextInputTokens`/`ContextCacheReadTokens` in the `result.Usage` → `TaskUsageEntry` conversion)<br>server/internal/daemon/usage_footprint_wiring_cerebro_test.go (net-new cerebro tripwire test) | 1 marked block + new cerebro test | FIR-1931 — the second hole behind the "estimate" context bar. The streaming runtimes (Claude et al.) compute the last-turn context footprint and stamp it onto the per-model usage map (`lastTurnFootprint.applyToMap` → `ContextInputTokens`/`ContextCacheReadTokens`, fixed for `[1m]`-tagged models in `cerebro_context_footprint.go`). But the daemon's `result.Usage` → `TaskUsageEntry` conversion copied only the four cumulative token columns, dropping the footprint at the report boundary — so `ReportTaskUsage` received `context_input_tokens=0`, `recordCerebroTaskContextFootprint` no-op'd, and every local Claude/`[1m]` session fell back to the cumulative sum (over-counts warm cache → pins at `~100% · 1000k/1000k · estimate`). Forwarding the two fields lets the server persist a real footprint so the bar reads true occupancy. Display-only, fully reversible, no DB change. The sibling server-side wiring already has a tripwire (`daemon-usage-wiring-guard`, FIR-2189); this adds the matching one for the daemon side, since the same upstream-sync deletions have repeatedly wiped usage wiring in this file. |

The claim-time injection + deny logic now lives in the cerebro helper `injectClaimConnectionMCP` (server/internal/handler/claim_connections_cerebro.go); the daemon.go patch is just the marked call. | `handler-connection-claim-resolver` + `handler-connection-claim-resolver-iface` + `claim-connections-cerebro` + `cerebro-connection-tool-resolver-claim-flip` | server/internal/handler/handler.go (1 marked struct field)<br>server/cmd/server/router.go (1 marked wiring block)<br>new cerebro files: server/internal/handler/claim_connections_cerebro.go, server/internal/cerebro/runtime/connection_tool_resolver_claim.go (+ _test) | 1 marked field + 1 marked wiring block + new cerebro files | FIR-2441 (the Flip) — the claim-time `mcp_http` injection is the fourth real access consumer to move onto the unified `runtime.ConnectionToolResolver`, after the brief (#2000), cloud executor (#2001) and local connectiontools handler (#2002). When the default-off `cerebro_api_connection_tools` flag is on, `ClaimConnectionMCP` returns the claim's `--mcp-config` **and** `--disallowedTools` deny tokens from ONE `Resolve()` (Allow-only raw relay entries per Lone #1 + deny from the same verdict table, so listed == callable). When the flag is off, `ClaimConnectionMCP` returns `handled=false` and `injectClaimConnectionMCP` falls back to the legacy `BuildMCPConfig`/`DisallowedMCPTools` path unchanged — that legacy branch is what keeps the swap reversible, since the `mcp_http` claim injection is a LIVE feature not otherwise behind the flag. Deleting the legacy branch (so `grep BuildMCPConfig server/` = nul in the decision path) makes the Allow-only rule the DEFAULT and is a separate reviewed step once the flag is permanently on. The router builds the claim resolver with a relay-aware entry builder so LOCAL runtimes get Multica relay URLs (FIR-1563), matching the legacy path. The agent-less runtime tool-scan (`runtime_tools_scan_cerebro.go`) deliberately STAYS on `BuildMCPConfig` — it is an inventory, not an access decision. |

The daemon carries them into `TaskContextForEnv.EffectiveTools`, and `cerebroToolsBrief` renders a deterministic, grouped "Connections & MCP tools" section into `## Available Commands` — so removing a permission drops the tool from the brief automatically (same resolution drives the list and live enforcement). | `daemon-claude-mcp-self-entry` | server/pkg/agent/claude.go (1 marked block in `Execute`, before the `--mcp-config` temp file is written) | 1 marked block (~8 lines) + 1 new helper func | FIR-2341 — `api`-type workspace connections (`infisical-admin`, `firtal-data-registry`, …) are served by `multica mcp serve`, a stdio MCP server the CLI already implements (FIR-2273) and that `multica mcp install` can register with Claude Code manually. But `buildClaudeArgs` unconditionally passes `--strict-mcp-config`, which makes Claude Code ignore every globally-registered server — so the manually-installed `multica` entry was never actually reachable from a daemon-spawned task, and when the workspace had zero `mcp_http` connections, `--mcp-config` was never passed at all (Claude Code got no MCP servers whatsoever). The fix merges a `{"mcpServers":{"multica":{"command":<self binary>,"args":["mcp","serve"]}}}` document into `opts.McpConfig` (via `daemonmcp.Merge`, so a user-authored server literally named `multica` still wins) on every Claude Code run, restoring `multica mcp serve` regardless of workspace connection config. `multica mcp serve` resolves its own `MULTICA_TOKEN`/`MULTICA_SERVER_URL`/`MULTICA_WORKSPACE_ID` from the environment Claude Code inherits (already set by the daemon on the parent process), so no new env plumbing was needed. `api`-type connection access control is unaffected — it is enforced server-side per-agent in the `/api/cerebro/connection-tools` list/call handlers (`connectiontools/handler.go`, FIR-2273), independent of Claude Code's `--disallowedTools`/`mcp__<server>__<tool>` deny scheme that only applies to `mcp_http` connections (`handler-connection-tool-deny`, TECH-3156). Scoped to the Claude Code provider only, matching `multica mcp install`'s `supportedMCPClients` — Codex/Cursor/Hermes/Kiro/Kimi render `opts.McpConfig` through their own translators and were never part of the `multica mcp install` design. |

The flag catalog (keys, labels, descriptions, defaults, groups) is generated from `packages/cerebro-feature-flags/registry.ts` by `pnpm generate:feature-catalog` and embedded in the binary via `go:embed`; effective values come from `GET /api/workspaces/{id}/feature-flags` and resolve with the same precedence as the frontend store (locked workspace > personal > workspace > default). Drift is blocked twice: a CI step re-runs the generator and diffs the JSON, and `packages/cerebro-feature-flags/catalog-sync.test.ts` compares the checked-in JSON against the live registry. |
| `agent-office-region-ci` | .github/workflows/ci.yml (1 marked step in the `upstream-zone-guard` job) | 2 marked lines | FIR-1775 §10 — CI write-gate for the Agent Office managed region in repo `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` files. The region (`<!-- agent-office:start vN sha256:... -->` … `<!-- agent-office:end -->`) is sealed with a SHA-256 of its body; `scripts/validate-agent-office-region.sh` rejects hand-edits (broken seal), region removal, and body changes without a version bump, while the human-owned rest of the file stays free. The canonical format lives in `server/internal/cerebro/agentoffice/managed_region.go`; the sanctioned write path is `multica agent context region-sync` (with `region-verify` for checks). Human override: `AGENT-OFFICE-ALLOW-REGION:` commit-message token or `AGENT_OFFICE_ALLOW_REGION_EDIT=1`. |
